  • In The Marvelous Land of Oz, Ozma is revealed to be Tip after Mombi’s restoration spell transforms him into the rightful Princess of Oz.
  • In The Marvelous Land of Oz: Princess Ozma.
  • In Ozma of Oz, Ozma leads a rescue expedition to free the royal family of Ev from the Nome King and returns triumphantly to Oz.
  • In Dorothy and the Wizard in Oz, Ozma welcomes Dorothy, presides over Eureka’s trial, releases Eureka after discovering the truth, and sends Dorothy home.
  • In The Road to Oz, Ozma prepares and hosts her birthday celebration, welcomes Dorothy and other guests, and arranges Dorothy’s return to Kansas.
  • In The Emerald City of Oz, Ozma compassionately and justly rules Oz, protects her people from the Nome invasion, and makes Oz invisible for its future protection.
  • In Little Wizard Stories of Oz, Ozma holds daily audiences, investigates troubles with the Wizard, and sends help to rescue Jack Pumpkinhead and lost children.
  • In The Patchwork Girl of Oz, Ozma rules justly and mercifully, regulates magic, judges Ojo’s case, and permits the restoration of Unc Nunkie and Margolotte.
  • In Tik-Tok of Oz, Ozma uses the Magic Picture and the Wizard’s magic to manage distant affairs, returns Ann Soforth’s people home, and welcomes new friends to Oz.
  • In The Scarecrow of Oz, Ozma uses the Magic Picture to identify endangered travelers, sends help, and welcomes Trot and Cap’n Bill to the Emerald City.
  • In Rinkitink in Oz: Ozma.
  • In The Lost Princess of Oz, Ozma is abducted and imprisoned by Ugu but is eventually freed from an enchanted golden peach pit and restored to the Emerald City.
  • In The Tin Woodman of Oz, Ozma uses magic to restore transformed travelers, defeats Mrs. Yoop’s enchantment, and settles the Tin Woodman’s affairs.
  • In The Magic of Oz, Ozma governs fairly, limits the practice of magic, celebrates her birthday, and insists that enchanted magicians be restored.
  • In Glinda of Oz, Ozma peacefully investigates the conflict between the Skeezers and Flatheads, rescues the Skeezers, and establishes lawful rule and peace.
  • In The Royal Book of Oz: Ozma.
  • In Kabumpo in Oz, Ozma survives the palace’s abduction, helps rescue and care for others, stops the Runaway Country, and deals with Ruggedo and Glegg’s magic.
  • In The Cowardly Lion of Oz, Ozma directs rescues and settles disputes, commands Mustafa to surrender, ends the Uns’ conflict, and offers Notta a home in Oz.
  • In Grampa in Oz: Ozma.
  • In The Lost King of Oz, Ozma searches for her father, is reunited with Pastoria, and becomes the acknowledged Queen of Oz.
  • In The Hungry Tiger of Oz, Ozma is abducted by Atmos Fere, survives the fall with him, helps him, and returns safely to the Emerald City.
  • In The Gnome King of Oz, Ozma protects her magical possessions from Ruggedo, later transports Peter and the pirate gold, and frees Ruggedo after judging his punishment sufficient.
  • In The Giant Horse of Oz, Ozma hears the travelers’ troubles, orders their aid, helps restore the sea horses, and appoints rulers and courtiers after a royal banquet.
  • In Jack Pumpkinhead of Oz, Ozma is captured by Mogodore but remains composed until her rescuers defeat him and restore her to power.
  • In The Yellow Knight of Oz: Ozma.

Tip in The Marvelous Land of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1904-06

Tip is a strong, rugged, mischievous youth, apparently a boy, raised and guarded by Mombi since quite young. He has no memory of parents, performs her chores, has no playmates, and openly hates her. When Mombi threatens to turn him into a marble statue, he flees with food and the Marvelous Powder of Life. Before and during this escape, he constructs Jack Pumpkinhead, becomes Jack’s creator and “father,” and uses the powder to bring the Saw-Horse to life. His practical ingenuity, care for his companions, and possession of the dwindling magic powder repeatedly matter throughout the journey. Traveling toward the Emerald City with Jack and the Saw-Horse, Tip is separated from them, meets General Jinjur, witnesses the capture of the city, and reunites with the Scarecrow’s party during its escape. He crosses the river, recovers Jack’s head, repairs Jack, helps settle disputes, and endures fatigue and hunger as the only plainly mortal traveler made of flesh, blood, and bone. He travels to the Tin Woodman, befriends both the Tin Woodman and the Scarecrow, recognizes Mombi’s witchcraft, and helps the group continue despite dangers and injuries. When imprisoned in the Emerald City, Tip explains the remaining power of the Powder of Life and helps create and animate the Gump, enabling flight. In the Jackdaws’ nest he directs the Gump, finds Dr. Nikidik’s wishing pills, uses one to undo his own wish, and later travels with Glinda, who takes a great liking to him. At the end, he learns that he is Princess Ozma, the rightful ruler of the Emerald City, not a boy born to Mombi. Though he initially wants to remain a boy, he accepts Mombi’s restoration spell, is transformed into Princess Ozma, and is thereafter a girl.

Narrative, section by section

22 sections; each heading opens the text

The Marvelous Land of Oz

Tip appears in the chapter list as the character who manufactures Pumpkinhead and makes an experiment in magic.

Tip Manufactures a Pumpkinhead

Tip is a strong, rugged youth raised by Mombi, whom he openly hates despite her being his guardian. While Mombi is away for two days, he makes a wooden man with a carved pumpkin head, dresses it in colorful clothing, and names it Jack Pumpkinhead.

The Marvelous Powder of Life

Tip carries Jack Pumpkinhead to a bend in the road, repairs his detached arm with a stronger pin, and turns his pumpkin head to face forward. He secretly watches Mombi sprinkle the Powder of Life over Jack and is amazed when Jack comes to life. Mombi threatens to transform Tip into a marble statue, but he stays awake rather than going to bed while she prepares the potion.

The Flight of the Fugitives

Tip decides to run away from Mombi rather than become a marble statue. He takes food and the Powder of Life, frees Jack Pumpkinhead, and travels with him toward the Emerald City.

Tip Makes an Experiment in Magic

Tip worries about Jack Pumpkinhead’s weak wooden joints and tries to find a way to remedy them. He uses the Powder of Life from the pepper-box on the saw-horse, imitating Mombi’s words and gestures, and successfully brings it to life.

The Awakening of the Saw-horse

Tip brings the Saw-Horse to life and tries to control it after it bumps into Jack Pumpkinhead and steps on Tip’s foot. He makes bark ears for the Saw-Horse, teaches it the meanings of “Whoa,” “Get-Up,” and “Trot,” and fastens a post to its back for Jack to hold. Tip then leads the group toward the Emerald City and camps beside the road at nightfall.

Jack Pumpkinhead’s Ride to the Emerald City

Tip resumes his journey with Jack Pumpkinhead and the Saw-Horse, crossing a river by riding the Saw-Horse. When he orders the Saw-Horse to trot, it races ahead, and Tip loses his grip on its tail and is left behind. He rests, then continues walking toward the Emerald City, expecting to overtake them at the city gates.

Gen. Jinjur’s Army of Revolt

Tip meets General Jinjur while walking toward the Emerald City and accepts food from her. He follows her to the Army of Revolt, carries baskets and packages for its members, and witnesses the capture of the Emerald City.

The Scarecrow Plans an escape

Tip follows the Soldier with the Green Whiskers into the Emerald City and reaches the palace. He warns the Scarecrow that the invading girls intend to make a rag carpet from his outside and stuff sofa-cushions with his inside. Tip rides at the front of the Saw-Horse during the escape, is slightly pricked by a knitting-needle, and ends up in the wide river.

The Journey to the Tin Woodman

Tip crosses the river on the Saw-Horse, retrieves Jack Pumpkinhead’s floating head, and helps repair both Jack and the Scarecrow. He mediates quarrels among his companions, then resumes the journey toward the Tin Woodman. At night, he is tired and hungry, so he sleeps on the grass using the Scarecrow as a pillow.

A Nickel-Plated Emperor

Tip travels with the Scarecrow, Jack Pumpkinhead, and the Saw-Horse to the City of the Winkies. He notices that Nick Chopper is made entirely of neatly soldered and riveted tin, and he tells Nick that the Saw-Horse was brought to life with magic powder. Tip mends Jack Pumpkinhead’s wooden limbs before the group begins its return journey to the Emerald City.

Mr. H. M. Woggle-Bug, T. E.

Tip identifies the sunflower field as witchcraft and suspects that Mombi is creating an illusion. He leads the group through it by having Jack ride forward while he follows the Saw-Horse with his eyes closed, and he later replaces Jack's pumpkin after the Saw-Horse falls.

A Highly Magnified History

Tip tells Pumpkinhead to keep quiet and later points out that insects do not normally wear clothes. He explains that the group cannot leave because the Saw-Horse is injured and Pumpkinhead must ride.

Old Mombi indulges in Witchcraft

Tip travels with the Scarecrow and the others toward the Emerald City. He is the only member of the party who becomes tired and must repeatedly stop to rest.

The Prisoners of the Queen

Tip travels with the Scarecrow's party into the Emerald City and warns that their unopposed progress is too easy and may conceal trouble. He is included among the prisoners whom General Jinjur intends to restore to old Mombi.

The Scarecrow Takes Time to Think

Tip confirms that Jinjur’s soldiers surround the palace and explains that his nearly exhausted Powder of Life can bring things to life. He agrees to use the powder on the winged machine and helps remove the Scarecrow’s crown.

The Astonishing Flight of the Gump

Tip helps bring the sofas to the roof and suggests using a second sofa as the machine’s body. He sprinkles the machine with magic powder, animates it with three cabalistic words, and later commands it to return to the roof.

In the Jackdaw’s Nest

Tip explains to the Gump that it is a Thing made to carry the travelers through the air. He directs the flight southward, discovers the wishing pills in the pepper-box, and later orders the Gump to land in the mountains.

Dr. Nikidik’s Famous Wishing Pills

Tip crawls out from under the sofas, helps the Woggle-Bug, and suggests using the money in the nest to restuff the Scarecrow. He swallows the first pill, suffers apparent pain, and wishes that he had never swallowed it, causing the pill to return to the box.

The Scarecrow Appeals to Glinda the Good

Tip secures a gold watch and jeweled decorations during the search of the Jackdaws' nest. He suggests that the Woggle-Bug use another Wishing Pill after the pepper-box is lost, and later identifies the South Country by its red houses, fences, and trees. Tip tells Glinda that Mombi is with Queen Jinjur in the Emerald City and helped hinder the travelers.

The Tin-Woodman Plucks a Rose

Tip travels with Glinda and the others and walks beside the Sorceress because she has taken a great liking to him. He rebukes the Woggle-Bug for making an unwanted joke and threatens to exclude him from their company.

Princess Ozma of Oz

Tip learns that he is Princess Ozma, the rightful ruler of the Emerald City, rather than a boy born to Mombi. He initially wishes to remain a boy but agrees to undergo Mombi's transformation spell and emerges as Princess Ozma.

Princess Ozma in The Marvelous Land of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1904-06

Princess Ozma is Pastoria’s daughter, the rightful heir and queen of the Emerald City. Hidden by the Wizard of Oz and transformed by Mombi’s magic into Tip while an infant, she is sought by Glinda, who needs help to restore her. Mombi reverses the enchantment, revealing Ozma as a beautiful young girl who remains emotionally the same person as Tip. After Jinjur’s defeat, Ozma becomes queen and rules the Emerald City with wisdom and justice despite her youth and inexperience. She returns stolen jewels, disbands the Army of Revolt, releases Jinjur, appoints the Scarecrow Royal Treasurer, and orders the Gump disassembled when it asks.

Narrative, section by section

5 sections; each heading opens the text

The Marvelous Land of Oz

Princess Ozma of Oz is named in the chapter list in a chapter titled with her transformation or appearance.

The Scarecrow Appeals to Glinda the Good

Ozma is identified by Glinda as Pastoria's daughter and the rightful heir to the throne of the Emerald City. The Wizard of Oz hid her in a secret place, and Glinda has been unable to discover where she is.

The Transformation of Old Mombi

Glinda says that Ozma will never be rescued and restored to her rightful position as Queen of the Emerald City without their assistance.

Princess Ozma of Oz

Princess Ozma is revealed to have been transformed into Tip when she was an infant. After Mombi reverses the spell, Ozma appears as a beautiful young girl and remains emotionally the same person as Tip.

The Riches of Content

Princess Ozma becomes Queen of the Emerald City after Jinjur's defeat and rules with wisdom and justice, aided by Glinda the Good and the Woggle-Bug. She orders the stolen jewels returned, disbands the Army of Revolt, releases Jinjur, and has the Gump taken apart at its request.

Ozma in Ozma of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1907

Ozma is the rightful heir and supreme ruler of the Land of Oz, a beautiful, delicate girl who lives in the Emerald City and rules under the title Ozma of Oz. After deposing General Jinjur, she governs as the former ruler’s only child. She possesses the ivory wand, a golden chariot, a magic carpet from Glinda the Good, a magic picture, and later the magic belt from Dorothy. Her central action is a generous rescue mission for the royal family of Ev, whom the Nome King holds captive. She crosses the desert in her chariot, frees Dorothy and her companions, consults Glinda, assembles an army, and leads the expedition into the Nome kingdom. Although she offers ornaments in exchange and threatens conquest, she accepts the Nome King’s challenge to identify the prisoners among his ornaments. After eleven guesses, she is transformed into an emerald grasshopper and becomes part of his collection. Billina restores her, and Ozma resumes command, orders her army against the Nome warriors, helps search for her missing companions, and refuses to leave without the Tin Woodman. She leads the rescued royal family and her party back to Ev and then returns to Oz, where she is welcomed and honors the adventurers. At the end, she entertains Dorothy, allows her to use the magic picture, accepts the magic belt for safekeeping, and promises to use it to bring Dorothy back to Oz when Dorothy signals.

Narrative, section by section

14 sections; each heading opens the text

front matter .. Author's Note

Ozma became the Ruler of Oz, and children asked what she did afterward. Some readers also asked Baum to have Ozma and Dorothy meet and enjoy themselves together.

5. Dorothy Opens the Dinner Pail

Ozma of Oz is described as the rightful heir to the throne of the Land of Oz. She deposed General Jinjur and now rules the land.

7. Ozma of Oz to the Rescue

Ozma of Oz crosses the desert in a golden chariot drawn by a Lion and a Tiger, leading a procession from the Land of Oz. She tells Langwidere that she is powerful enough to destroy her kingdom but has come to free the royal family of Ev from the Nome King, and she orders Langwidere to release Dorothy.

8. The Hungry Tiger

Ozma has set Dorothy and her friends free and invites Tiktok to sit in the drawing-room. She announces that a solemn conference will decide how to liberate the royal family of the Land of Ev.

9. The Royal Family of Ev

Ozma learns that Evoldo sold his family to the Nome King and decides that the prisoners must still be liberated. She consults Glinda the Good, receives a magic carpet, assembles an army, and leads the expedition toward the Kingdom of the Nomes.

10. The Giant with the Hammer

Ozma stops the chariot for Billina, uses the magic carpet to cross a deep gulf, and questions Tiktok about the iron giant. She rides the Cowardly Lion beneath the hammer and later helps oversee the rescue of the private.

11. The Nome King

Ozma of Oz leads her companions to the Nome King's palace to rescue the Queen of Ev and her ten children. She first demands and requests the Nome King's appearance, then offers replacements for the royal family and threatens to conquer his kingdom before agreeing to his guessing challenge.

12. The Eleven Guesses

Ozma accepts the Nome King's offer and enters his royal palace to identify the royal family of Ev among its ornaments. After ten unsuccessful guesses, she makes an eleventh guess blindly and is transformed into a pretty grasshopper formed from a single emerald.

16. Purple, Green, and Gold

Ozma of Oz has been transformed into an emerald grasshopper, but Billina restores her to her lovely and dainty form. Ozma helps search for the Scarecrow, Tiktok, and the Tin Woodman and decides that the group must make the Nome King explain what happened to their friend.

17. The Scarecrow Wins the Fight

Ozma is restored to her natural form during Billina’s mission. She orders her army to fight the Nome warriors, and the generals pass the order down until a private soldier attacks.

18. The Fate of the Tin Woodman

Ozma welcomes the rescued people and refuses to leave without finding the Tin Woodman. She leads the returning party and orders the march through the passage after the search fails.

19. The King of Ev

Ozma returns to Ev in her golden chariot with the Queen mother, the royal children, and their companions. She welcomes the Tin Woodman when Billina restores him and is splendidly entertained by the Queen mother at the palace.

20. The Emerald City

Ozma leads the return journey from Ev to the Land of Oz on her magic carpet. She is welcomed as the ruler of Oz, receives a grand reception, honors the adventurers, and promotes Omby Amby to command the armies and her Body Guard.

21. Dorothy's Magic Belt

Ozma entertains Dorothy in the Land of Oz and lets her use the magic picture. She agrees to help Dorothy return to Uncle Henry and accepts the magic belt so it will not be lost. She is to watch for Dorothy's signal in the magic picture and use the belt to bring Dorothy back to Oz.

Princess Ozma in Dorothy and the Wizard in Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1908

Princess Ozma is a girl, princess, and youthful ruler of Oz, the Land of Oz, and the Emerald City, universally loved by the people of Oz. Formerly named Tip, she was transformed from a boy by Mombi’s enchantment. She explains that Oz traditionally means “Great and Good” and was the ruler’s name, while Ozma was used for a woman ruler. As ruler, Ozma uses and safeguards powerful magic. She owns the Powder of Life, with which she brought the Sawhorse, and once the Gump, to life, and she possesses the Nome King’s Magic Belt, kept in Oz because it does not work in the United States. She also has an enchanted picture in her room, through which she promises to watch Dorothy daily and bring her to Oz if needed. She uses the Belt to transport Dorothy, Eureka, and their companions to the Emerald City, welcomes Oz, and appoints him Official Wizard of her kingdom. Ozma’s rule combines ceremony, affection, and judgment. She orders a city holiday for visitors, rides with Dorothy in a grand procession, enjoys games with her people, names the victorious Sawhorse Prince of Horses, and keeps a white piglet as a pet. When the piglet disappears, she orders Eureka arrested, sets a court, appoints a defender and accuser, and presides over the trial from the emerald throne. Though she initially prepares to execute Eureka after a guilty verdict, she learns her piglet was trapped in a vase, recovers it, and releases Eureka. At the end, she allows Dorothy to use the enchanted picture, sends Dorothy and Eureka to Kansas with the Magic Belt, and arranges Zeb and Jim’s return to Hugson’s Ranch.

Narrative, section by section

7 sections; each heading opens the text

14. Ozma Uses the Magic Belt

Princess Ozma rules the Land of Oz and is a friend of Dorothy. She has promised to look at Dorothy through an enchanted picture every day at four o'clock and to use the Nome King's Magic Belt to bring her to Oz if she needs help. She transports Dorothy and Eureka first and then brings the remaining travelers to the Emerald City.

15. Old Friends are Reunited

Princess Ozma welcomes Oz and offers him a home for the rest of his life as the Official Wizard of her kingdom. She explains that Oz was traditionally the name of the ruler of the Land of Oz, meaning "Great and Good," while Ozma was used when the ruler was a woman. She also tells Oz how the four Witches divided the kingdom and how Mombi transformed her into a boy.

16. Jim, The Cab-Horse

Princess Ozma brought the Sawhorse to life with magic powder, fitted his legs with gold plates, and likes him. She also once sprinkled the Gump with the Powder of Life, and she later appears to greet Dorothy and invite her to breakfast.

17. The Nine Tiny Piglets

Princess Ozma orders a holiday throughout the Emerald City in honor of her visitors and rides in the grand procession with Dorothy. She keeps one of the tiny piglets as a pet, later laughs with her people during the games, and names the victorious Sawhorse Prince of Horses.

18. The Trial of Eureka the Kitten

Ozma discovers that her white piglet is missing and suspects that Eureka ate it. She orders Eureka’s arrest, announces that the Court will meet at three o’clock, and appoints the Tin Woodman as defender and the Woggle-Bug as Public Accuser.

19. The Wizard Performs Another Trick

Princess Ozma presides over Eureka's trial from the emerald throne and initially prepares to have Eureka executed after the jury finds her guilty. She learns that the Wizard's piglet is only a substitute and that her own piglet is trapped in a vase; after the vase is broken, she recovers her pet and releases Eureka.

20. Zeb Returns to the Ranch

Ozma allows Dorothy to use the enchanted picture and later uses the Magic Belt to send Dorothy and Eureka to Kansas. She also arranges for Zeb and Jim to return to Hugson's Ranch.

Princess Ozma in The Road to Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1909

Princess Ozma is the princess and girl Ruler of Oz, ruling the Land of Oz, including the Emerald City and all four countries. She is Dorothy's friend, a gracious and beautiful ruler who inspires love rather than fear or ordinary admiration; at her banquet she is admired as dignified and bewitching. She possesses the Magic Picture, through which she watches travelers, and the Magic Belt, used for rescue and transport. She also owns the royal chariot, though she chooses not to ride it during her public birthday procession. Her past includes living with Mombi in the shape of a boy, fleeing with a pepper-box containing the Powder of Life, and witnessing that Powder bring Jack Pumpkinhead to life. Ozma built Jack's body, carved his pumpkin head, and brought him to life with Magic Powder, so Jack considers her his parent. She later used the remaining Powder of Life to bring the Flying Gump to life. The records chiefly follow preparations for Ozma's birthday. While busy organizing the celebration, she watches Dorothy and her companions, sends Tik-Tok and Billina to welcome them, and sends the Cowardly Lion and Hungry Tiger to meet Dorothy because she may be weary. She has arranged roads to guide Dorothy toward Oz, invites Dorothy to the Emerald City, and prepares a welcome for her friends and guests. She orders luxurious apartments, clothing, ornaments, and comforts for the shaggy man, eagerly welcomes Dorothy, and expects her friends for dinner after rest. Ozma invites rulers and guests to a grand party and banquet, asks Dorothy to receive arrivals while she oversees preparations, and receives many birthday presents. As honored hostess, she greets the guests at the royal banquet, where they drink to her health and happiness. On the anniversary of her birth, she leads a public procession and entertainment outside the Emerald City, walking with subjects and guests and receiving their good wishes. At the end, she agrees that Dorothy and Toto will be transported to Kansas while Dorothy sleeps.

Narrative, section by section

12 sections; each heading opens the text

3. A Queer Village

Princess Ozma is Dorothy's friend and is known by the foxes as an important personage. She is preparing to celebrate her birthday on the twenty-first of the month.

7. The Shaggy Man's Transformation

Princess Ozma is Dorothy's friend and is expected to hold a grand birthday celebration. Dorothy plans to ask her to invite King Kik-a-bray if Dorothy reaches her.

14. Tik-Tok and Billina

Princess Ozma sees Dorothy and her companions in her magic picture and sends Tik-Tok and Billina to welcome them. She is too busy preparing for her birthday celebration to come herself.

16. Visiting the Pumpkin-Field

Ozma made Jack Pumpkinhead's body and carved his pumpkin heads, bringing him to life with the Magic Powder. Jack considers Ozma his parent and intends to attend her party.

17. The Royal Chariot Arrives

Ozma once lived with Mombi in the shape of a boy and witnessed the Powder of Life bring Jack Pumpkinhead to life. She later used the remaining Powder of Life to bring the Flying Gump to life, and the travelers are going to her party.

18. The Emerald City

Ozma sends the Cowardly Lion and Hungry Tiger to meet Dorothy because she fears Dorothy will be weary from her long walk. She is expecting Dorothy and her friends for dinner after Dorothy has rested and changed, and her birthday is the day after tomorrow.

19. The Shaggy Man's Welcome

Princess Ozma orders luxurious apartments and clothing, ornaments, and other comforts prepared for the shaggy man. She waits eagerly in the drawing-room to welcome Dorothy.

20. Princess Ozma Of Oz

Ozma welcomes her friends and guests to her palace and is described as exceptionally beautiful, charming, gracious, and loved rather than feared. She arranged the roads to guide Dorothy toward Oz, watched Dorothy's journey in the Magic Picture, and invited King Dox of Foxville and King Kik-a-bray of Dunkiton to her celebration. She decides that the Love Magnet should hang over the gates of the Emerald City and announces that her guests will arrive the next day for her birthday celebration.

21. Dorothy Receives the Guests

Ozma is busy ordering preparations for the next day's festivities and asks Dorothy to receive the invited guests in her place. Her birthday presents, including a carolite necklace and a gingerbread crown, are placed together in the palace.

22. Important Arrivals

Princess Ozma is the honored host of the birthday celebration and has invited Santa Claus and many other guests. She receives presents including gifts from Santa Claus, the Queen of Merryland, the Braided Man, the royal family of Ev, and Queen Zixi, King Bud, and Princess Fluff.

23. The Grand Banquet

Ozma hosts the royal banquet in honor of her birthday. She enters in person, greets her guests, and is admired for being both dignified and bewitching. The guests drink to her health and happiness, and the banquet is followed by plans for her birthday celebration the next morning.

24. The Birthday Celebration

Princess Ozma celebrates the anniversary of her birth with a grand public procession and entertainment in the green fields outside the Emerald City. She walks with her subjects and guests instead of riding in her chariot, receives their good wishes, and later agrees that Dorothy and Toto will be transported to Kansas while Dorothy sleeps.

Ozma in The Emerald City of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1910

Ozma is the beautiful, beloved fairy-powered Princess and ruler of the Land of Oz, living in the Emerald City palace and governing all its countries. Her magical resources include a fairy wand, the Magic Belt, and the Magic Picture; she also has the Cowardly Lion and Hungry Tiger as bodyguards and favors the Sawhorse as a steed. What matters most is her compassionate, just care for Oz and its people: she attends to their welfare, settles disputes fairly, welcomes and houses Uncle Henry and Aunt Em, gives Dorothy her rightful rank as Princess of Oz, and supports Dorothy's journeys and requests. Ozma's rule faces danger from Roquat the Red and the Nomes, who seek to destroy or enslave her and conquer Oz. Although she had previously led a conquest against the Nomes after they enslaved the Royal Family of Ev, she learns through the Magic Picture that Roquat has built a tunnel into Oz and refuses military resistance, believing Oz cannot defeat the invaders. She will neither abandon her people nor save herself while they are enslaved, choosing instead to share their fate and meet the attackers peacefully at the Forbidden Fountain. There she identifies the amnesiac Nome King as Roquat, persuades him to send his army home, and uses the Magic Belt to return the other invaders to their homes. Concerned that future outsiders may enter by tunnels or other routes, she seeks Glinda's help and agrees to make Oz invisible, separating it from the outside world for protection.

Narrative, section by section

17 sections; each heading opens the text

1. How the Nome King Became Angry

Princess Ozma rules the Land of Oz and has fairy powers that General Blug says would make the Nome army helpless. Roquat intends to destroy her or enslave her, but she does not know about his plan or that he remains alive under the mountains.

2. How Uncle Henry Got Into Trouble

Ozma is the girl Ruler of the Land of Oz and Dorothy's faithful friend. She has made Dorothy a Princess and has often asked Dorothy to live in the Emerald City. Through her Magic Picture, Ozma sees Dorothy every day at four o'clock and can summon her using the Magic Belt when Dorothy makes the secret sign.

3. How Ozma Granted Dorothy's Request

Ozma listens sympathetically to Dorothy's request and agrees to invite Uncle Henry and Aunt Em to live in the Land of Oz. She plans to provide them with a comfortable place where they need not work more than they wish. Ozma decides to use the Magic Belt to transport them to the Emerald City of Oz without warning them.

4. How The Nome King Planned Revenge

Ozma is the Princess who rules Oz and possesses a fairy wand. Guph says that King Roquat and the Nomes would have no chance against her without additional magical power.

5. How Dorothy Became a Princess

Princess Ozma welcomes Dorothy back and tells her to assume her rightful rank as a Princess of Oz. She uses the Magic Belt to bring Uncle Henry and Aunt Em from Kansas, receives them graciously in the Throne Room, and asks the people of Oz to show them kindness and honor.

7. How Aunt Em Conquered the Lion

Ozma requires Aunt Em and Uncle Henry to live in the palace and has royal dressmakers prepare clothing for Aunt Em. She provides for the people and animals in the Land of Oz, including Billina's chickens, and has the Cowardly Lion and the Hungry Tiger serve as her body guards.

8. How the Grand Gallipoot Joined The Nomes

Ozma is described by Guph as a kind and good ruler of the Land of Oz. She previously marched against the Nomes with an army after they enslaved the Royal Family of Ev.

9. How the Wogglebug Taught Athletics

Ozma notices that Uncle Henry and Aunt Em are uneasy without light tasks and proposes that Dorothy take them on a journey while she searches for suitable occupations. She provides an escort and prepares the party to leave the next morning.

13. How the General Talked to the King

Ozma is claimed by Roquat the Red as one of the prisoners he intends to capture. He plans to transform her into a china ornament and says he will prevent the invading creatures from hurting or enslaving her.

18. How Ozma Looked into the Magic Picture

Princess Ozma manages the affairs of the Land of Oz and thinks of occupations for Uncle Henry and Aunt Em. She uses the Magic Picture to watch Dorothy and her companions, discovers Roquat the Red's tunnel project, and recognizes the danger it poses to the Land of Oz.

19. How Bunnybury Welcomed the Strangers

Ozma of Oz gives Dorothy a letter instructing her subjects to greet Dorothy with the same courtesy and consideration they would extend to Ozma. The Keeper recognizes Ozma's authority and obeys the command.

24. How the Tin Woodman Told the Sad News

Ozma discovers the tunnel through her Magic Picture and informs the Tin Woodman that it is nearly complete. She refuses the Tin Woodman's offer to bring the Winkies to her assistance because she believes all the inhabitants of Oz together are not powerful enough to defeat the Nome King's forces.

25. How the Scarecrow Displayed His Wisdom

Ozma is threatened by the Nome King's plan to conquer and destroy Oz. She has said that her friends can do nothing to oppose their enemies, but the Scarecrow decides to stand by her and offer his sympathy when the disaster occurs.

26. How Ozma Refused to Fight for Her Kingdom

Ozma learns that the Nome King's tunnel is complete and that the Nome King, the Whimsies, the Growleywogs, and the Phanfasms intend to attack the Emerald City at daybreak. She firmly refuses to fight, abandon her people, or use violence even to save her kingdom, and she plans to meet the invaders peacefully at the Forbidden Fountain.

27. How the Fierce Warriors Invaded Oz

Ozma of Oz and her people are threatened by the invasion. The Nome King and General Guph believe their vengeance against her is about to be accomplished.

28. How They Drank at the Forbidden Fountain

Ozma joins the others at daybreak and explains that the invaders are approaching through the dusty tunnel. She tells the amnesiac Nome King that he is Roquat, King of the Nomes, and persuades him to order his army home before using the Magic Belt to return the other invaders to their homes.

29. How Glinda Worked a Magic Spell

Ozma becomes concerned that airships, sand-boats, tunnels, and other routes could allow outsiders to reach the Land of Oz. She visits Glinda the Good to seek a way to protect Oz and gratefully accepts Glinda's completed Magic Spell.

Princess Ozma in Little Wizard Stories of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1913

Princess Ozma is the beautiful, powerful princess and ruler of Oz, living in Emerald City and governing from a glistening-emerald throne in the Throne Room, where the Cowardly Lion and Hungry Tiger serve as chief guardians. Concerned above all with her people’s happiness, she holds a daily audience to hear their troubles and uses the Magic Picture in the Royal Palace to learn of events across Oz. She is Tiktok’s favorite ruler, studies Tiktok, and is feared by the Nome King. With the Wizard, she journeys through Oz to investigate complaints, confronts mischievous Imps, and approves keeping their spirits in buttons in hopes of reforming them. When the Magic Picture reveals lost children, she sends her friend Jack Pumpkinhead with a map; after Jack is harmed, she sends the little Wizard, riding the Cowardly Lion, to rescue Jack and the children.

Narrative, section by section

4 sections; each heading opens the text

THE COWARDLY LION AND THE HUNGRY TIGER

Princess Ozma is the Ruler of Oz and sits on a throne of glistening emeralds in the Throne Room. The Cowardly Lion and Hungry Tiger are her chief guardians.

TIKTOK AND THE NOME KING

Princess Ozma of Oz is Tiktok's powerful ruler and favorite, and the Nome King fears that she will be angry when he destroys Tiktok. The King expects her to send an army against him, but instead resolves to send Tiktok back to her with jewels.

OZMA AND THE LITTLE WIZARD

Princess Ozma rules the Land of Oz and wants all its people to be happy, so she travels with the Wizard of Oz to investigate complaints. She confronts the three mischievous Imps, orders the Wizard to use magic against them, and later agrees that keeping their spirits in buttons may reform them.

JACK PUMPKINHEAD AND THE SAWHORSE

Ozma sees the lost little girl and little boy in the Magic Picture and sends Jack Pumpkinhead to rescue them. After Jack's head is smashed, she sends the little Wizard mounted on the Cowardly Lion to save Jack and the children.

Ozma in The Patchwork Girl of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1913-07-01

Ozma is the powerful, just, merciful, and rightful girl Ruler and Princess of the Land of Oz, ruling as its sole ruler. Queenly in person and character but also a real girl, she is wise, makes no mistakes in judgment, and is joyous, light-hearted, and free in her private apartments. Her rule centers on protecting Oz: she restricts magic, forbids unlicensed practice by Dr. Pipt, makes and enacts laws, and issues an edict rendering Oz invisible to those outside its borders and ending future communication with the outside world, while permitting the Royal Historian to receive and record news of Oz. She possesses a Magic Picture that shows scenes of interest throughout Oz. She is Dorothy Gale's very best friend, keeps Dorothy in her palace, and brought Uncle Henry and Aunt Em to Oz. She also welcomes and trusts friends including the Scarecrow, Shaggy Man, Wizard, Jack Pumpkinhead, and others. Her treatment of Ojo shows her justice. After learning that he picked a six-leaved clover, an act prohibited without her consent because it could enable harmful magic, she orders his arrest and imprisonment. Yet she intends prisoners to become strong and brave, promises a just and merciful trial, and ultimately forgives Ojo and allows him to keep the clover. She permits Dr. Pipt, under supervision, to make a charm restoring Unc Nunkie and Margolotte from marble statues. Once they are restored, she removes Dr. Pipt's magic and sorcery, destroys his four kettles, and burns his recipe book. At the end, she permits the Wonderful Wizard of Oz to obey Glinda the Good's commands, keeps the Glass Cat as a palace pet, and gives Ojo and Unc Nunkie a house outside the Emerald City under her protection.

Narrative, section by section

9 sections; each heading opens the text

The Patchwork Girl of Oz / Prologue

Ozma of Oz issued the edict that would render Oz invisible to those outside its borders and cut off future communication with the outside world. She graciously consented to Dorothy sharing the latest news of Oz with the Historian.

Chapter Thirteen

Ozma is described as the powerful Ruler of the Land of Oz, whose law forbids picking a six-leaved clover without her consent. She possesses a Magic Picture that can show scenes anywhere in the Land of Oz, although she may not always be watching it. The Scarecrow says she may not allow the Crooked Magician to restore Unc Nunkie because he practices magic without a license.

Chapter Fourteen

Ozma is the Ruler of Oz and Princess Ozma is the gracious ruler whose orders the Soldier with the Green Whiskers follows. She has apparently ordered Ojo's arrest for willfully breaking one of the Laws of Oz.

Chapter Fifteen

Ozma knows that Ojo picked the six-leaved clover and orders him imprisoned for breaking a Law of Oz. Tollydiggle says Ozma believes that someone who commits a fault is not strong and brave enough, and that she will be just and merciful when Ojo is tried.

Chapter Sixteen

Ozma of Oz is Dorothy Gale's best friend and keeps Dorothy in her palace. She brought Dorothy's Uncle Henry and Aunt Em to Oz and ordered Ojo's arrest; Dorothy expects her to decide what will happen to him. Ozma had also forbidden Dr. Pipt from practicing magic.

Chapter Seventeen

Ozma welcomes her old and trusted friends to a private banquet, where she behaves as a joyful, light-hearted girl rather than only as a dignified ruler. She discusses the Patchwork Girl, the Woozy, and the Powder of Life, and tells how she became the rightful Ruler of Oz.

Chapter Eighteen

Ozma judges Ojo's offense and explains that the law against picking six-leaved clovers was made to prevent harmful magic. She forgives Ojo, permits him to keep the clover, and agrees to let Dr. Pipt make a charm to restore Unc Nunkie and Margolotte.

Chapter Twenty-Seven

Ozma agrees that the Tin Woodman was right to forbid taking a yellow butterfly's wing and says she would have warned Ojo that the ingredient could never be obtained. She reveals that Dr. Pipt has been brought to the palace, his four kettles have been destroyed, and his recipe book has been burned. She also says that marble statues of Unc Nunkie and Margolotte are in the next room and leads the party to them.

Chapter Twenty-Eight

Ozma permits the Wonderful Wizard of Oz to obey Glinda the Good's commands and decides to keep the Glass Cat as a palace pet. She gives Ojo and Unc Nunkie a nice house outside the Emerald City, where they will live under her protection.

Ozma of Oz in Tik-Tok of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1914

Ozma is the gentle, beloved ruler of the Land of Oz, living in the Emerald City and placing her subjects first. Taller than Dorothy and Betsy, she governs without force: her tiny army is unarmed, and she once removed her sole soldier’s gun. Though initially unaware of Ann Soforth’s planned invasion while caring for her people, she can use the Magic Picture to observe distant events. Near the end, she uses the Wizard’s magic to return Queen Ann and the people of Oogaboo home, then admits Shaggy Man, his brother, Betsy, and Hank when Shaggy will not leave them. She welcomes the newcomers, values friendship over rivalry, reconciles Hank with the animals, and explains Oz’s spell on animal speech.

Narrative, section by section

4 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter One

Princess Ozma rules the Land of Oz from the Emerald City and is loved and obeyed by her people. Her army is said to consist of twenty-seven unarmed officers, and she once made her only private soldier a Captain-General and took away his gun.

Chapter Three

Princess Ozma is busy caring for her subjects and is unaware of Ann Soforth's plan to invade the Land of Oz.

Chapter Twenty-Four

Ozma uses the Wizard's magic to send Queen Ann and the people of Oogaboo back to their home. After learning that Shaggy Man will not return to Oz without his new companions, she decides to admit Shaggy Man, his brother, Betsy, and Hank to the Land of Oz.

Chapter Twenty-Five

Ozma welcomes Betsy and Hank to the Land of Oz and explains that friendship is more important than rivalry. She reconciles Hank with the other animals and reveals that the spell of Oz affects animals' ability to speak.

Ozma in The Scarecrow of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1915

Ozma is the young girl and fairy Ruler of the Land of Oz, descended from Fairy Queens, who rules every king and subject from her royal palace in the Emerald City. Loved as a comrade and protector, she is wise, sympathetic, generous, truthful, and honorable. Using her Magic Picture, she sees travelers endangered in Jinxland, alerts the Wizard to aid them, and sends Dorothy and Betsy Bobbin to meet them. She later welcomes Trot and Cap’n Bill, provides rooms, hosts a banquet, and reassures Trot about Button-Bright.

Narrative, section by section

2 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter Twenty-One

Ozma is the girl Ruler of the Land of Oz and lives in her palace in the Emerald City. After seeing Trot, Cap'n Bill, and Button-Bright in the Magic Picture, she worries about their safety in Jinxland, has the Wizard prepare a house for them, and allows Dorothy and Betsy Bobbin to go meet them.

Chapter Twenty-Four

Ozma sends Dorothy and Betsy Bobbin to invite Trot and Cap'n Bill to her palace. She gives the newcomers rooms and hosts a grand banquet and reception in their honor, assuring Trot that Button-Bright cannot go far astray in Oz.

Ozma in Rinkitink in Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1916

Ozma is a lovely girl ruler of the Land of Oz, known for usual hospitality, banquets, and a Magic Picture that shows desired scenes. She permits Dorothy to travel to the Nome Country, advises taking the Wizard of Oz, lends the Magic Carpet, and watches over her. After learning Inga's parents are liberated, she prepares a banquet, provides royal clothing, and welcomes the returning guests.

Narrative, section by section

2 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter Twenty

Ozma permits Dorothy to go to the Nome Country and advises her to take the Wizard of Oz. She lends Dorothy the Magic Carpet and uses the Magic Picture to watch over Dorothy while she is away.

Chapter Twenty Two

Ozma learns through her Magic Picture that Inga's parents have been liberated and orders a splendid banquet for the returning party. She provides new royal clothing for Queen Garee, King Kitticut, King Rinkitink, and Prince Inga, and welcomes the guests to her palace.

Ozma in The Lost Princess of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1917

Ozma is the lovely, beautiful, beloved girl ruler, princess, and powerful fairy who is the Supreme Ruler and protector of the Emerald City and all the Land of Oz. She lives in her royal palace, rules its people, loves Dorothy, values Glinda the Good as a servant, and is guarded by the Cowardly Lion on state occasions. Her treasures include the Magic Picture, which can reveal people approaching her palace with intent to conquer it, and she wears a chain of emeralds. She forbids magic to all subjects except Glinda and the Wizard of Oz. She cannot be destroyed by any magic, though she can be overpowered and confined. Ozma disappears suddenly from her royal apartments in the Emerald City without telling Dorothy or her friends. Her loss causes widespread grief, and friends and travelers search for her through places including the City of Thi, where she is not found. They suspect she has been stolen, eventually believing that Ugu the Shoemaker took her along with magic from the Emerald City. Ugu confronts Ozma in her apartments, takes her magical possessions, binds her in the Magic Dishpan, carries her to the Wicker Castle, and imprisons her. Although she remains the ruler of Oz despite Ugu's claim, he hides her so completely that even the Magic Picture shows only a round black spot. At last, Ozma is discovered confined within a golden peach pit in Button-Bright's pocket. The Wizard opens the pit, and she emerges from a pink cloud-like haze, freed from enchantment. After hearing how her friends conquered Ugu, she thanks those who helped, invites the Bear King to visit her palace, confiscates Ugu's magical possessions, and returns triumphantly to the Emerald City. Restored before the dishpan is returned, she gives Cayke the Cookie Cook the diamond-studded gold dishpan.

Narrative, section by section

18 sections; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER 1

Princess Ozma is the lovely girl ruler of the Land of Oz and a powerful fairy. She disappears from her royal apartments without telling Dorothy or any of her other friends where she has gone.

CHAPTER 2

Ozma rules the Land of Oz from the Emerald City and regards Glinda the Good as a valued servant. She suddenly disappears, and no one in the Emerald City knows what has become of her.

CHAPTER 5

Ozma cannot be found anywhere in the Emerald City, and her friends conclude that she may have been overpowered and taken captive by an enemy. She is the fairy Ruler of the Land of Oz and is regarded by its inhabitants and her friends as their protector.

CHAPTER 6

Ozma has been stolen, causing widespread grief and prompting several search parties to look for her. The travelers do not find any news of her in the district they cross.

CHAPTER 9

Royal Ozma is not being held in the City of Thi, and the High Coco-Lorum says that the Thists have never heard of her. The travelers continue their search for her elsewhere.

CHAPTER 10

Ozma remains missing, and the travelers are trying to find her. Toto believes that the same person who stole his growl may have stolen Ozma.

CHAPTER 11

Ozma remains missing while the travelers search for her. Dorothy fears that Ozma may be imprisoned in a dungeon, and the group intends to rescue her if she is found.

CHAPTER 12

Ozma is the Supreme Ruler of the Land of Oz and is missing. The travelers suspect that Ugu the Shoemaker may have stolen her and intend to search his castle.

CHAPTER 14

Ozma is described as a dear little girl and a fairy who rules the Emerald City, the Emperor of the Winkies, and all the Land of Oz. The Winkie woman says Ozma may be able to identify the person who took Cayke's dishpan.

CHAPTER 17

Ozma has been stolen, and Dorothy's party believes Ugu took her along with the magic in the Emerald City. Her Magic Picture is in Ugu's room and allows the travelers to see him watching them.

CHAPTER 18

Ozma was stolen by Ugu the Shoemaker and shut up in a dark place. The travelers believe this place is a dungeon cell and make rescuing her their immediate purpose.

CHAPTER 19

Ozma of Oz is the fairy ruler of the Emerald City and the Land of Oz, and no magic can destroy her. Ugu confronts her in her apartments, takes her magical possessions, binds her in the Magic Dishpan, and carries her to the Wicker Castle, where he makes her a prisoner.

CHAPTER 20

Ozma of Oz remains missing and is believed to have been stolen by Ugu the Shoemaker. The companions search for her, but they do not find her in the hole in the ground, and her actual location remains a mystery.

CHAPTER 22

Ozma of Oz is not present in the central hall. Ugu says that he has hidden her and refuses to tell the group where she is, while the Wizard and his followers continue to intend to rescue her.

CHAPTER 23

Ozma remains hidden, and the group tries to use the Magic Picture to locate her. Instead of showing her, the Picture displays a round black spot.

CHAPTER 24

Ozma is discovered inside the golden peach pit in Button-Bright's pocket. She emerges from a pink, cloud-like haze after the Wizard opens the pit and thanks her friends.

CHAPTER 25

Ozma is released from the golden peach pit and listens as Dorothy, Trot, Button-Bright, the Wizard, and Betsy explain how they conquered Ugu. She thanks the Frogman and the little Pink Bear, invites the Bear King to visit her palace, confiscates Ugu’s magical possessions, and returns triumphantly to the Emerald City.

CHAPTER 26

Ozma is found and restored to the Emerald City. She gives Cayke the Cookie Cook the diamond-studded gold dishpan after receiving it from the Scarecrow and the Tin Woodman.

Ozma in The Tin Woodman of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1918

Ozma is a maiden, girl ruler, and fairy who rules the Land of Oz wisely and kindly from her palace in the Emerald City. She knows much magic, possesses a small silver Wand and a Magic Room, and uses magic only for her subjects' benefit; she also forbids unauthorized magic. A friend of Dorothy, the Tin Woodman, and others, she watches over Winkie Country and protects Woot the Wanderer as she is able. After Mrs. Yoop transforms travelers, Ozma studies how to break the enchantments and travels with Dorothy toward Jinjur's Ranch. With her wand she restores the Scarecrow from a bear and the Tin Woodman from an owl. She works through successive transformations to restore Polychrome, but initially says that known magic cannot restore Woot from the Green Monkey form. She then accepts Polychrome's proposal to exchange Woot's form with Mrs. Yoop's. Using magical broth as a mirror, she observes Mrs. Yoop and performs the transformation: Mrs. Yoop becomes Woot and then the Green Monkey, while Woot returns to his natural form, defeating the Giantess. Ozma later welcomes Woot to her palace, offers to introduce him to Ojo the Munchkin and Button-Bright, supports the Tin Woodman's search for Nimmie Amee, and offers a grand Emerald City wedding if Nimmie Amee wishes it. She carries the travelers to the Munchkin forest, entertains their return, settles the matter of Nimmie Amee and Chopfyt, assigns Captain Fyter to serve in Gillikin Country, and promises to watch over Woot's journeys.

Narrative, section by section

5 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter Twelve

Ozma rules the Land of Oz and uses her magic only to benefit her subjects. After seeing Mrs. Yoop transform the travelers, she studies ways to break the enchantments and sets out with Dorothy toward Jinjur's Ranch to help them.

Chapter Thirteen

Ozma of Oz uses her silver Wand and magic to restore the Scarecrow and the Tin Woodman to their original forms. She experiments with several transformations before restoring Polychrome, but explains that she cannot remove the Green Monkey form from Woot by known magic. After Polychrome's proposal, Ozma agrees to try giving Mrs. Yoop Woot's original form so their forms can be exchanged.

Chapter Fourteen

Ozma prepares a magical broth, uses it as a mirror to observe Mrs. Yoop, and performs the transformation that changes Mrs. Yoop into Woot and then into the Green Monkey. She restores Woot the Wanderer to his natural form and triumphs over the Giantess.

Chapter Fifteen

Ozma tells Woot the Wanderer that he may visit her palace, where she will introduce him to Ojo the Munchkin and Button-Bright. She agrees that the Tin Woodman may continue looking for Nimmie Amee and offers to hold a grand wedding at the Emerald City if Nimmie Amee still wishes to marry him. She carries the travelers in the Red Wagon to the edge of the great Munchkin forest before returning to the Emerald City with Dorothy and Toto.

Chapter Twenty-Four

Ozma warmly entertains the returning travelers at her palace and advises them that Chopfyt need not be considered because Nimmie Amee is content with him. She arranges for Captain Fyter to serve as a soldier in the Gillikin Country and promises to watch over Woot's future journeys.

Ozma in The Magic of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1919

Ozma is the beautiful, beloved fairy girl ruler of the Land of Oz and the Fairyland of Oz. She rules all Oz countries from her palace in the center of Emerald City, and her people love and admire her for sweetness, gentleness, and fairness. Dorothy, Trot, and Betsy Bobbin are devoted friends; she also has royal courtiers, Maids of Honor, and animal friends. Her rule is marked by care, restraint, and responsibility. She decrees that only Glinda the Good and the Wizard of Oz may practice magic in her dominions; Bini Aru obeys, while Kiki Aru knowingly breaks the decree. She permits Dorothy to use the Wooden Sawhorse and Red Wagon, hears Professor H. M. Wogglebug's appeal to punish the rebellious Senior Class, and seems unlikely to be severe because she herself refused to replace ordinary food with Square-Meal Tablets. The records center on preparations for Ozma's coming birthday: a grand Royal Palace feast, songs, gifts, an emerald court gown, Dorothy's surprise cake, and a cake planned to release twelve trick-performing monkeys. Dorothy, Trot, and Cap'n Bill arrange the Magic Flower, which Ozma receives with particular delight at the celebration, alongside the emerald gown, friends, and animals. At the end, Ozma insists that enchanted magicians be restored from unnatural forms despite possible danger. When Kiki Aru and Ruggedo lose their memories, she keeps them in Oz so they can learn to be good, true, and considerate, rather than relearn evil ways.

Narrative, section by section

9 sections; each heading opens the text

1. Mount Munch

Ozma of Oz rules the Land of Oz and decreed that only Glinda the Good and the Wizard of Oz could practice magic in her dominions. Bini Aru obeyed her decree, while Kiki Aru knowingly disobeyed it by using the transformation method.

5. A Happy Corner of Oz

Ozma of Oz rules the Land of Oz and is loved and admired by its people. Her birthday is next month, and Dorothy, Trot, and Cap'n Bill plan to give her a Magic Flower.

6. Ozma's Birthday Presents

Ozma is preparing to celebrate her birthday with a grand feast at the Royal Palace. Her friends plan to give her gifts including a song, a tin girdle, straw slippers, an emerald court gown, and Dorothy's surprise cake. She grants Dorothy permission to use the Wooden Sawhorse and Red Wagon but is not told about Dorothy and the Wizard's secret journey.

11. The Beasts of the Forest of Gugu

Ruggedo claims that Ozma, who rules the Land of Oz, told her people that the beasts were lazy and useless and should be captured and made to work as slaves.

16. The Glass Cat Finds the Black Bag

Ozma is to receive a birthday cake featuring twelve small monkeys hidden inside it. When she cuts the cake, the monkeys are meant to jump onto the table and perform tricks.

19. Dorothy and the Bumble Bees

Ozma is intended to receive the Magic Flower as a birthday present from Trot and Cap'n Bill. Dorothy and Trot expect the unusual flower to surprise her and everyone in the Emerald City.

21. The College of Athletic Arts

Ozma heard Professor H. M. Wogglebug, T.E., appeal for punishment of the rebellious Senior Class. She had refused to eat Square-Meal Tablets in place of ordinary food, so she was probably not severe with the students.

22. Ozma's Birthday Party

Ozma is the beautiful fairy Ruler of the Fairyland of Oz and is greatly beloved for being sweet, gentle, and fair. She celebrates her birthday with a feast, welcomes friends and animals, receives many gifts, and is especially delighted by the Magic Flower and an emerald gown.

23. The Fountain of Oblivion

Ozma insists that the enchanted magicians should be freed from their unnatural forms, despite the danger they may pose. After Kiki Aru and Ruggedo lose their memories, she decides to keep them in the Land of Oz and teach them to be true and considerate of others.

Ozma in Glinda of Oz

L. Frank Baum · 1920

Ozma is a fairy, Princess, and the lawful, supreme ruler of the Land of Oz, including its countries, Emerald City, Skeezers, and Flatheads. Appointed under Fairy Queen Lurline, she lives in Emerald City and Ozma's palace, commands from a throne, and is beloved by her subjects. Her silver wand, tipped with a sparkling emerald, is her chief instrument of defense and magic; she has great fairy powers, though she cannot use witchcraft and is less powerful in magic than Glinda the Sorceress. Most important to her rule are freedom, happiness, peace, and protection for her subjects. She dislikes war and believes using power to make others happy brings happiness to herself. Learning that the Skeezers and Flatheads are her subjects and nearing war, she refuses threats and travels peacefully with Dorothy, leaving the Scarecrow to rule in her absence. Along the way she uses her wand to overcome dangers, cross the mist, make camp, discover a route around an invisible wall, and escape the Flatheads after the Su-dic imprisons her. She seeks to remove the Su-dic's unlawful power and aid the Skeezers, but Queen Coo-ee-oh rejects Ozma's authority and has her held prisoner. Ozma investigates the Skeezers' unhappiness and Coo-ee-oh's magic, then becomes trapped with Dorothy and the Skeezers in the submerged island's Great Dome. Her council, Glinda, the Wizard, and others undertake a rescue. After they reach the village, Ozma helps identify how to raise the island. She finally requires the Skeezers to recognize her lawful rule, approves Lady Aurex as Queen, plans punishment for the Su-dic, protects the Skeezers from future harm, and returns to the Emerald City after peace and loyalty are secured.

Narrative, section by section

20 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter One

Ozma learns that the Skeezers and Flatheads are her subjects and decides that her duty is to prevent them from fighting. She rejects sending the Wizard of Oz to threaten them and chooses to travel peacefully with Dorothy to settle their dispute.

Chapter Two

Ozma asks the Scarecrow to rule the Land of Oz while she travels with Dorothy toward the Skeezer Country. She uses her Silver Wand to repel the Spider King and change a green crab white, and escapes the purple spiders with Dorothy.

Chapter Three

Ozma looks down into the mist-filled valley with Dorothy and decides they must venture into the mist if they wish to continue. She calls for the Mist Maidens and asks them to take her and Dorothy to the opposite hillside. The Mist Maidens carry her across, and she thanks them.

Chapter Four

Ozma travels with Dorothy and uses her silver wand to create a handsome, well-appointed tent for their night’s camp. She explains that unlimited magic would remove the pleasure of striving and earning, and says that using her powers to make others happy gives her happiness.

Chapter Five

Ozma explains that her magic cannot create a horse and wagon or an automobile, though she says Dorothy possesses the magical ability to win people’s hearts. She recognizes the purpose of the invisible wall, finds the way around it, and uses her silver wand to illuminate the dark passage. Ozma also hears the warning bell and the Flatheads’ alarmed voices before reaching the summit with Dorothy.

Chapter Six

Princess Ozma identifies herself as the Ruler of Oz and tells the Su-dic that her laws apply to both the Flatheads and the Skeezers. She seeks to prevent war and proposes restoring Rora to human form with one can of brains while returning the other three cans to those Rora robbed. When the Su-dic orders her imprisoned, she vanishes with Dorothy.

Chapter Seven

Ozma makes herself and Dorothy invisible with her silver wand and leads Dorothy out of the Flatheads’ stronghold. After escaping, she decides to find the Lake of the Skeezers and help them resist the Flatheads. She believes the Flathead people may become peaceful if the Su-dic is removed from power and deprived of his unlawful magic.

Chapter Eight

Princess Ozma signals the Skeezers with her handkerchief and crosses to Skeezer island with Princess Dorothy on a retractable steel bridge. She explains that she rules the Land of Oz under authority from the Fairy Queen Lurline and tries to persuade Queen Coo-ee-oh to avoid war with the Flatheads. Queen Coo-ee-oh rejects her authority and orders her kept prisoner until the battle is over.

Chapter Nine

Ozma uses her silver wand to enchant Lady Aurex's house so that Queen Coo-ee-oh cannot hear anyone speaking within it. She gains Lady Aurex's trust, learns the history of the three fishes, and seeks to understand why the Skeezers are unhappy.

Chapter Ten

Ozma says she is glad to have learned about the Skeezers and Flatheads because her task is to liberate them and secure their freedom and happiness. She believes she and Dorothy are not in danger despite the island's submergence and the approaching conflict.

Chapter Twelve

Ozma realizes that Coo-ee-oh's transformation has placed the Skeezers, their island, Dorothy, and herself in grave danger. She takes possession of Coo-ee-oh's palace, investigates the witchcraft room, and promises to study its contents in an attempt to save everyone.

Chapter Thirteen

Ozma reaches the Enchanted Mountain of the Flatheads and escapes with Dorothy to the Magic Isle of the Skeezers. She is later reported to be a prisoner in the great Dome of the Isle of the Skeezers beneath the lake.

Chapter Fourteen

Princess Ozma has gathered a council of unusual but trustworthy advisers. She is in danger, and the council decides to travel to the Skeezer country to rescue her.

Chapter Fifteen

Ozma is the ruler whom the rescue expedition sets out to help. The Cowardly Lion, one of her regular guardians, says he will help her.

Chapter Seventeen

Ozma remains imprisoned beneath the Great Dome on the submerged Magic Isle. Glinda and the others seek to rescue her without abandoning the imprisoned Skeezers.

Chapter Twenty

Ozma is seen safely inside the Great Dome with Dorothy. She recognizes Glinda and the Wizard through the submarine's windows, signals with them, and smiles to encourage their rescue efforts.

Chapter Twenty-One

Princess Ozma is the appointed Ruler of Oz. She is imprisoned in the Skeezer village inside the sunken island.

Chapter Twenty-Two

Ozma is living in the Queen's palace and welcomes the Wizard and the others when they reach the village. She explains her efforts to prevent war between the Flatheads and the Skeezers and says that she cannot raise the island because she does not understand Coo-ee-oh's unusual magic.

Chapter Twenty-Three

Ozma recognizes the steel basin as the upper end of the pillar supporting the island. She approves Dorothy's idea, suggests that ee operates the bridge, and says the company should go down to join the people after the island is raised.

Chapter Twenty-Four

Princess Ozma demands that the Skeezers recognize her as their lawful Ruler and obey the laws of the Land of Oz, promising to protect them from future harm. She approves Lady Aurex as Queen, plans to punish the Su-dic, and returns to the Emerald City after helping secure peace and loyalty among the Skeezers and the former Flatheads.

Ozma in The Royal Book of Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1921

Ozma is a little girl and Queen of Oz, its lovely ruler, descended from a long line of fairies. She is friends with the Scarecrow, possesses and can use the Magic Picture, and is known as a fair ruler. At her palace party, she permits Professor Wogglebug to begin compiling the Royal Book and hears that her family tree is Oz’s oldest and most illustrious. Later, after learning that the Horners and Hoppers are at war again, she travels with Betsy Bobbin, Jack Pumpkinhead, and the Wizard of Oz to settle it; with Glinda, she stops the war. She gives emerald collars to the A-B-Sea Serpent and Rattlesnakes. Finally, she welcomes Dorothy, the Scarecrow, Sir Hokus, and the animals back to Oz, seeks the Magic Belt’s help with the magic fan, and holds a celebration.

Narrative, section by section

3 sections; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER 1

Ozma is hosting a party at her palace when Professor Wogglebug arrives with his idea. She permits him to begin compiling the Royal Book and listens as he questions the guests. The Professor says her family tree is the oldest and most illustrious in Oz because she is descended from a long line of fairies.

CHAPTER 10

Princess Ozma discusses Dorothy with Betsy Bobbin and hears from a messenger that the Horners and Hoppers are at war again. She orders Jack Pumpkinhead to harness the Sawhorse and tells the Wizard of Oz to prepare for a journey, then travels with Betsy Bobbin, Jack Pumpkinhead, and the Wizard of Oz to help settle the war. She gives the A-B-Sea Serpent and Rattlesnakes emerald collars and does not use the Magic Picture to watch Dorothy and the Scarecrow.

CHAPTER 24

Princess Ozma welcomes Dorothy, the Scarecrow, Sir Hokus, and the animals back to Oz. She sends for the Magic Belt when Dorothy explains the problem with the magic fan and later gives a party celebrating their return. She also tells the gathering how she and Glinda stopped the war between the Horners and Hoppers.

Ozma in Kabumpo in Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1922

Ozma is the little fairy Princess, Queen, and Fairy Ruler of Oz, ruling from her royal Emerald City palace. She is described as the loveliest, beautiful, lovely, and good; she has lived almost a thousand years, is unharmed by dangers because she is a fairy, and is regarded by Kabumpo as the Proper Princess. As ruler, she welcomes Trot and Betsy Bobbin to remain in Oz, directs Scraps to live in the Emerald City, and is someone Sir Hokus seeks permission from before his quest. Her palace, with Ozma and its important occupants, is carried away when Ruggedo becomes a giant, creating her disappearance from the Emerald City and terrifying Oz. In Ev, she is pinned beneath a collapsed bed but is freed by the Scarecrow and Scraps, helps tend the injured, marks a warning sign with her name and Oz's Royal seal, and prevents Sir Hokus from killing Ruggedo because his fall could destroy the palace. The Sand Man then puts her into enchanted sleep. Glinda brings her down after the Question Box reveals a rescue plan; Ozma tries Trick Tea and refuses Prince Pompadore's proposal. She nevertheless helps Pompadore's party, uses the Question Box and magic spinning to stop the Runaway Country, intends to banish Ruggedo there, and sends the party to the Winkie Country. Learning Ruggedo's confession, she uses her Magic Belt to transport him. She also takes and keeps Glegg's Question Box and Mixed Magic, uses the Belt to bring Glegg to the Emerald City, and follows the Box's instruction that Glegg drink Triple Trick Tea.

Narrative, section by section

11 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter 4

Ozma of Oz is described as the loveliest little Fairy Princess and the ruler of the Emerald City. Kabumpo believes she is the Proper Princess whom Pompadore should marry, although Ozma does not know that Pumperdink exists.

Chapter 7

Ozma is the little fairy Princess who rules Oz. Sir Hokus intends to ask her permission before starting his giant quest, and she has invited Trot and Betsy Bobbin to remain in Oz and has insisted that Scraps live with the other celebrities in the Emerald City.

Chapter 8

Ozma disappears along with her palace and the other important people inside it. The inhabitants are terrified by the loss of their Queen.

Chapter 9

Ozma’s palace is accidentally carried away when Ruggedo grows into a giant. The palace’s movement causes Ozma and her guests to be tossed about whenever Ruggedo shakes his head.

Chapter 12

Princess Ozma has disappeared from the Emerald City along with everyone in the palace. Glinda discovers that Ozma is in Ev after a giant carried her away, and Pompa resolves to rescue her.

Chapter 13

Ozma is described by Peg Amy as beautiful, lovely, and good. She has disappeared, and Peg Amy and the others are traveling to Ev to help her. Prince Pompadore hopes she is the Proper Princess he must marry.

Chapter 14

Ozma is pinned beneath the collapsed bed in her apartment but is unharmed. The Scarecrow and Scraps free her, and she helps care for the injured occupants and paints her name and the Royal seal of Oz on the warning sign. She orders the windows closed and warns Sir Hokus not to kill Ruggedo, since Ruggedo's fall could destroy the palace.

Chapter 15

Ozma is looking out of a palace window with Dorothy and the others when the Sand Man spills his silver sand. She falls into the enchanted sleep and slips down beside Betsy Bobbin and Trot.

Chapter 18

Ozma is brought down from her palace by Glinda after the Question Box provides a plan to save her. She agrees to try the Trick Tea and then refuses Pompadore’s marriage proposal.

Chapter 19

Ozma refuses to marry Prince Pompadore but agrees to help him and his companions. She uses the Question Box to stop the Runaway Country, plans to punish Ruggedo by banishing him to its island, and arranges for Prince Pompadore's party to travel to the Winkie Country.

Chapter 22

Ozma learns from Ruggedo’s confession how her palace came to be impaled on the spikes of his gray head. She uses her Magic Belt to transport Ruggedo and later asks the Question Box how to deal with Glegg, following its direction to make him drink Triple Trick Tea.

Ozma in The Cowardly Lion of Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1923

Ozma is the little fairy Queen and ruler of Oz and the Emerald City. Kind and authoritative, she possesses a magic belt and a Magic Picture. In the palace, she silences a dispute while playing checkers with the Scarecrow, questions Notta Bit More about traveling with a witch, and accepts the Wizard of Oz’s proof that Notta is not a witch. After learning of the Cowardly Lion’s danger, she orders a rescue party to Mudge, where Notta transports her first. She commands Mustafa to surrender his ring, decrees that Crunch remain in Mudge as a monument, observes the Uns with Glinda and the Wizard, summons Unselfish to end their battle, and offers sad Notta a permanent happy home in Oz.

Narrative, section by section

5 sections; each heading opens the text

Chapter 18

Princess Ozma is in the palace when Bob Up, Notta, Nick, and their captors enter the throne room. She is playing checkers with the Scarecrow and raises her scepter to silence the shouting.

Chapter 19

Ozma questions Notta about traveling with a witch and is relieved when the Wizard of Oz proves that Notta is not a witch. After Glinda reports the Cowardly Lion's danger, Ozma orders her Magic Belt brought so that a rescue party can travel to Mudge, but Notta touches her and transports her there first.

Chapter 20

Ozma arrives at the lion enclosure with the Scarecrow, Notta Bit More, Bob, and others. She is recognized as Princess Ozma of Oz by Tazzywaller.

Chapter 21

Ozma calls for someone to stop Crunch and later blames Mustafa for what has happened. She orders Mustafa to surrender his ring and declares that Crunch will remain forever in Mudge as a monument.

Chapter 22

Ozma uses the Magic Picture with Glinda and the Wizard to observe the Uns and summons Unselfish from hiding to end their battle. She recognizes that Notta is sad about leaving and invites him to stay in Oz, promising that he will not have an old age there.

Ozma in Grampa in Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1924

Ozma is a wise little girl fairy and ruler of all Oz, governing what is permitted or forbidden from her palace in the Emerald City. Prince Tatters plans to visit her court. She believes Dorothy is visiting the Tin Woodman, then follows Dorothy’s adventures with a magic picture and hears Grampa’s story on the Wizard of Oz’s radio. Using her magic belt and the wizard’s magic, she transports the group safely to Ragbad, permanently restores Fumbo’s head, and sends Dorothy, Toto, Peer Haps, and the Forgetful Poet home.

Narrative, section by section

2 sections; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER 3

Princess Ozma is the ruler of all Oz and lives in a palace in the Emerald City. Prince Tatters looks forward to visiting her and being presented at her court.

CHAPTER 22

Ozma follows Dorothy’s adventures through a magic picture and hears the story with the Wizard of Oz’s radio. Using her magic belt and the wizard’s magic, she transports the group safely to Ragbad, restores Fumbo’s head permanently, and later sends Dorothy, Toto, Peer Haps, and the Forgetful Poet home.

Ozma in The Lost King of Oz

Ruth Plumly Thompson · 1925

Ozma is the little fairy Queen and peaceful, prosperous ruler of Oz, descended from the Fairy band and daughter of the King of Oz. As a child, she and her father hid from Mombi at the castle of Morrow. Mombi transformed Ozma into the boy Tip, but Glinda forced Mombi to reverse the enchantment, and Ozma was proclaimed Queen. She has a palace in the Emerald City, is Dorothy's friend, and is among the few allowed to practice Mombi's magic. When messages suggest that her father may be alive, Ozma recalls Morrow and eagerly seeks him. Mombi remains a threat, planning to turn Ozma into a piano. Ozma disappears from the palace garden with her companions, then leads her faithful followers back toward the Emerald City. Expecting to find her father, she is devastated when the royal castle has vanished; when it rises again, she is carried up on one of its domes. At the restored castle, she finds that the supposed King is still a dummy and helps investigate his identity. She is finally reunited with Pastoria, who places the emerald crown on her head, making her ruler of Oz. In the final procession she rides the Saw Horse, then decides, with the King's agreement, to wash Mombi away with water.

Narrative, section by section

10 sections; each heading opens the text

CHAPTER 2

Ozma is the little girl ruler of Oz and the daughter of King Kinda Jolly. Mombi had transformed her into a boy named Tip, but Glinda forced Mombi to disenchant her, after which Ozma was proclaimed Queen of Oz.

CHAPTER 5

Snip intends to tell Ozma the entire story so that she can use her magic to help Pajuka find the King. Pajuka calls Ozma a pretender, while Snip notes that she does not know her father is alive.

CHAPTER 8

Ozma watches Scraps, thinks about Dorothy's absence, and receives the golden message with alarm. She asks what the message means and later recognizes that Morrow may be a kingdom in Oz.

CHAPTER 9

Ozma recognizes Morrow and remembers having visited the castle many times. She identifies it as the hunting lodge where she and her father hid from Mombi when she was a little girl. After learning that her father may be alive, she becomes delighted and wants to return to the Emerald City to find him.

CHAPTER 15

Ozma is in danger from Mombi, who plans to transform her into a piano. Dorothy insists that this must not happen and intends to warn her.

CHAPTER 17

Ozma is absent from the palace, having vanished from the garden with the little group that was with her. Her household searches for her, leaving the palace without attendants when Dorothy and the others arrive.

CHAPTER 18

Ozma journeys with her faithful followers toward the Emerald City and is overjoyed to learn that her father, the King of Oz, is in the palace. She hurries toward the capital but arrives to find the royal castle gone, then throws herself onto the grass and weeps. When the castle bursts from the ground, she is carried into the air on one of its rounded domes.

CHAPTER 19

Ozma arrives at the restored castle with her companions and anxiously asks where her father, the King, is. She is disappointed and bewildered when she discovers that he is still a dummy.

CHAPTER 20

Ozma helps investigate the identity of the lost King and hopes that Sir Hokus may be her father. She is reunited with Pastoria and becomes ruler of Oz after he places the emerald crown on her head.

CHAPTER 21

Ozma rides the famous Saw Horse in the grand procession. Afterward, she considers what to do with Mombi and decides to wash her away with water after receiving the King's agreement.
